matrix once appropriate basis sets are chosen. Its finite-dimensional (truncated or finite-rank)
approximation is then obtained by selecting a finite number of basis functions in each space.

J.1
Infinite-Dimensional Matrix Form of the Operator

Since

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

are Hilbert spaces with inner products
{\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle}_{\mux}
and
{\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle}_{\muy}
respectively, we can choose orthogonal bases for both spaces:
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}=\{\phi_{i}\mid\phi_{i}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}=\{\psi_{j}\mid\psi_{j}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\}_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. This choice allows any function
f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
to be represented by their infinite-dimensional coefficient vectors
{\bm{\alpha}}=[{\langle\phi_{i},f\rangle}_{\mux}]_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{\bm{\beta}}=[{\langle\psi_{j},h\rangle}_{\muy}]_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
, so that:

\small f({\bm{x}}):=f_{{\bm{\alpha}}}({\bm{x}})=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}{\langle\phi_{i},f\rangle}_{\mux}\,\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\equiv{\bm{\alpha}}^{T}\bm{\phi}({\bm{x}})\hskip 18.49988pth({\bm{y}}):=h_{{\bm{\beta}}}({\bm{y}})=\sum_{j=1}^{\infty}{\langle\psi_{j},h\rangle}_{\muy}\,\psi_{j}({\bm{y}})\equiv{\bm{\beta}}^{T}\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})

(55)

Here,
{\bm{\alpha}}^{T}\bm{\phi}({\bm{x}})
and
{\bm{\beta}}^{T}\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})
represent the function as the dot product of its expansion coefficients with the basis evaluations
\bm{\phi}({\bm{x}})=[\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})]_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})=[\psi_{j}({\bm{y}})]_{j\in\mathbb{N}}
. This notation is useful when we later select a finite number of basis functions to form a finite-dimensional approximation of
{\mathsf{T}}
.

With the chosen bases, the action of a linear integral operator
{\mathsf{T}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
on any
f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
is determined by its action on the basis functions:

\small[{\mathsf{T}}f_{{\bm{\alpha}}}]({\bm{y}})=\int_{{\mathcal{X}}}\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\Bigl(\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha_{i}\,\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\Bigr)\mux(d{\bm{x}})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha_{i}\int_{{\mathcal{X}}}\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\,\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\mux(d{\bm{x}})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha_{i}\,[{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{i}]({\bm{y}})

(56)

Since
[{\mathsf{T}}\phi_{i}]\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, each
[{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{i}]({\bm{y}})
can be expanded using the output basis as

[{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{i}]({\bm{y}})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\langle\psi_{j},\,{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{i}\rangle}_{\muy}\,\psi_{j}({\bm{y}}).

Thus, the operator
{\mathsf{T}}
can be represented by the infinite-dimensional matrix
{\bm{T}}
with entries

{\bm{T}}_{ij}={\langle\psi_{i},\,{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{j}\rangle}_{\muy}.

Therefore, the action of
{\mathsf{T}}
on any
f_{{\bm{\alpha}}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
is given by the matrix–vector product
{\bm{\beta}}={\bm{T}}\,{\bm{\alpha}}
, i.e.,

\small\begin{split}[{\mathsf{T}}f_{{\bm{\alpha}}}]({\bm{y}})&=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha_{j}\,[{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{j}]({\bm{y}})=\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\alpha_{j}\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}{\langle\psi_{i},\,{\mathsf{T}}\,\phi_{j}\rangle}_{\muy}\,\psi_{i}({\bm{y}})\\[2.84526pt]
&=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bm{T}}_{ij}\,\alpha_{j}\,\psi_{i}({\bm{y}})\equiv({\bm{T}}\,{\bm{\alpha}})^{T}\,\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})\end{split}

(57)

Eq.

57
shows that knowing the action of
{\mathsf{T}}
on the bases
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
determines its action on any function in

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

. In the sections that follow, we describe how symmetry constrains this action by requiring the bases to be
\G
-stable and by imposing
\G
-equivariance on
{\bm{T}}
, thereby introducing exploitable algebraic constraints for improved finite-rank approximations.

J.1.1
\G
-Equivariant Matrix Form of the Operator

Whenever the function spaces carry a symmetry group
\G
, the group action on their bases
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
is defined by the unitary representations

\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}:\G\to\mathbb{U}(){\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}}
and
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}:\G\to\mathbb{U}(){\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}}

(see
Definition

I.3
). As in
Equation

57
, these representations can be expressed in (infinite-dimensional) matrix form so that the group action is given by a matrix-vector product:

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\alpha}}](\cdot)

\displaystyle\equiv(\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{\alpha}})^{T}\bm{\phi}(\cdot),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\;f_{\bm{\alpha}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},g\in\G

(58)

\displaystyle[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}h_{\bm{\beta}}](\cdot)

\displaystyle\equiv(\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{\beta}})^{T}\bm{\psi}(\cdot),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\;h_{\bm{\beta}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{},g\in\G

Since the operator
{\mathsf{T}}
is
\G
-equivariant by construction (
Equation

54a
), the matrix form
{\bm{T}}
of the operator must also be
\G
-equivariant with respect to the group representations
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
:

\displaystyle f_{\bm{\alpha}}]]({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}[{\mathsf{T}}f_{\bm{\alpha}}]]({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle\forall\;f_{\bm{\alpha}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},g\in\G,{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{Y}}

(59)

\displaystyle({\bm{T}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{\alpha}})^{\top}\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=(\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{T}}{\bm{\alpha}})^{\top}\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle\text{s.t. \lx@cref{creftypeplural~refnum}{eq:matrix_representation_operator} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eq:group_action_basis_xy}}

\displaystyle{\bm{T}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g)

\displaystyle=\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{T}}

With bases
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
for

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

, the kernel (
Definition

J.1
) can be written as
\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=\sum_{i,j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bm{T}}_{i,j}\,\phi_{j}({\bm{x}})\,\psi_{i}({\bm{y}})
. Hence, the
\G
-invariance condition (
Equation

54b
) on the kernel directly implies that the matrix
{\bm{T}}
is
\G
-equivariant, as stated in the following proposition:

Proposition J.2

(
\G
-invariant kernel implies
\G
-equivariant matrix form)
.

Let
{\mathsf{T}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be a
\G
-equivariant operator between symmetric function spaces endowed with the group actions
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}
and
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}
of a compact symmetry group
\G
. Let
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
be the group representation of the on the input/output function spaces on the chosen basis sets
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
. Then the
G
-invariance of the operator’s kernel function (
Equation

54b
) implies that the matrix form of the operator, in the chosen basis sets, is
\G
-equivariant w.r.t the group representations
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
(
Equation

59
).

Proof.

The proof follows by choosing appropriate
\G
-stable basis sets
\{\phi_{i}\}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\{\psi_{j}\}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, so that for all
g\in\G
we have
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{i}=\phi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i}
and
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}\psi_{j}=\psi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}
with
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i,g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j\in\mathbb{N}

7
7

7

By Cayley’s theorem
(
Cayley, 1854
)
, every finite symmetry group is isomorphic to a permutation group. From a representation-theoretic perspective, this guarantees the existence of a permutation (regular) basis for any finite group.
. This basis sets the
\G
-invariance of the kernel translates into algebraic constraints on the matrix form
{\bm{T}}
.

\displaystyle k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\pmd(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\bm{x}},g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\bm{y}})\hskip 18.49988pt\forall\;g\in\G,{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{X}},{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{Y}}

(60)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bm{T}}_{i,j}\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\psi_{j}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bm{T}}_{i,j}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{i}]({\bm{x}})[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}\psi_{j}]({\bm{y}})=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}{\bm{T}}_{i,j}\phi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i}({\bm{x}})\psi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}({\bm{y}})

That is, the kernel is
\G
-equivariant if the operator’s matrix satisfies

{\bm{T}}_{i,j}={\bm{T}}_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i,\,g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}

for all

g\in\G,\;i,j\in\mathbb{N}.

This condition exactly characterizes the
\G
-equivariance of the matrix form.

\displaystyle{\bm{T}}_{i,j}={\langle\psi_{i},{\mathsf{T}}\phi_{j}\rangle}_{\muy}

\displaystyle={\langle\psi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i},{\mathsf{T}}\phi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}\rangle}_{\muy}={\bm{T}}_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i,g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}

\displaystyle\forall g\in\G,i,j\in\mathbb{N}

(61)

\displaystyle={\langle g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}\psi_{i},{\mathsf{T}}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{j}]\rangle}_{\muy}

\displaystyle={\langle g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}\psi_{i},g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}[{\mathsf{T}}\phi_{j}]\rangle}_{\muy}

\displaystyle\text{s.t. \lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:equivariant_operator}}

\displaystyle={\langle\psi_{i},{\mathsf{T}}\phi_{j}\rangle}_{\muy}={\bm{T}}_{i,j}

\displaystyle\text{s.t. \lx@cref{creftype~refnum}{eq:symmetry_action_on_f_space_unitarity}}

∎

J.1.2
Block-Diagonal Structure of the Operator Matrix Form

According to
Thm.

H.17
, a Hilbert space with a compact symmetry group
\G
decomposes into
{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}
orthogonal subspaces—one for each irreducible representation type—yielding an orthogonal decomposition of the operator’s input and output spaces:

\small\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}^{\perp}_{1\leq{k}\leq{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},\hskip 18.49988pt\text{and}\hskip 18.49988pt\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}^{\perp}_{1\leq{k}\leq{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{},

(62)

where

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

denote the
{k}
-th isotypic subspaces of

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

, respectively. Such that any function in these spaces can be decomposed into a sum of its projections onto the isotypic subspaces:

\small f({\bm{x}})=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\bm{x}}),\hskip 9.24994pth({\bm{y}})=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}h^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\bm{y}})\hskip 9.24994pt\text{with }\hskip 9.24994ptf^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},h^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}.

(63)

The orthogonal decomposition of the function spaces implies there exist unitary operators
{\mathsf{A}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\rightarrow\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
{\mathsf{B}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\rightarrow\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
(with matrix forms
{\bm{A}}
and
{\bm{B}}
), that describe a change of basis from the canonical basis to an
isotypic basis
,
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}=\cup_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}={\mathsf{A}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}

and

{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}=\cup_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}={\mathsf{B}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
, where the group’s representations decompose into a direct sum of representations per isotypic subspace (see
Definition

H.6
):

\small\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}(\cdot):={\bm{A}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(\cdot){\bm{A}}^{*}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(\cdot)\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}(\cdot):={\bm{B}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(\cdot){\bm{B}}^{*}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(\cdot).

(64)

Then, denoting the matrix form of
{\mathsf{T}}
in the isotypic basis by
{\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}={\bm{B}}^{*}{\bm{T}}{\bm{A}}
, the
\G
-equivariance of
{\mathsf{T}}
results in the matrix form of the operator in the isotypic basis being block-diagonal, with each block being
\G
-equivariant with respect to the group representations on the isotypic subspaces:

\displaystyle\small{\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}(g){\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}(g^{-1})

(65)

\displaystyle=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g^{-1})

\displaystyle\text{s.t. \lx@cref{creftypeplural~refnum}{eq:op_equivariance_matrix_representation_xy} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{eq:group_representations_isotypic_xy}}

\displaystyle{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g){\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g^{-1}),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall\;{k}=1,\ldots,{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{T}}^{(1)}&&\\
&\ddots&\\
&&{\bm{T}}^{({n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}})}\end{bmatrix}.

Each
{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
represents the matrix form of the operator
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
in the isotypic basis, acting on the isotypic subspaces of type
{k}
in the input and output spaces. This shows that
\G
-equivariant operators preserve the structure of isotypic subspaces without mixing functions from different types.

This property is crucial for the finite-rank approximation of the operator
{\mathsf{T}}
, as it reduces the problem to approximating lower-rank operators
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, for
{k}\in[1,{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
. Moreover, the block diagonal structure of
{\bm{T}}^{\text{\rm iso}}
allows us to rewrite
Equation

57
in the isotypic basis in terms of the action of each
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
on the projection
f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
of the function onto the
{k}^{\text{th}}
isotypic subspace, see (
63
), such that:

\displaystyle({\bm{y}})=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}[{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{y}})\equiv\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}({\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{\alpha}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}})^{\top}\bm{\psi}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\bm{y}}).\hskip 18.49988pt\bm{\psi}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}(\cdot)=[\psi_{j}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}(\cdot)]_{j\in\mathbb{N}},\forall\;\psi_{j}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\in{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}.

(66)

In the isotypic basis
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}=\cup_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
, the expansion coefficient vector
{\bm{\alpha}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\bm{\alpha}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
is formed from the projections of
f
onto each isotypic subspace:
{\bm{\alpha}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=[{\langle\phi_{i}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}},f\rangle}_{\mux}]_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
. The block-diagonal structure of
{\bm{T}}^{{\text{\rm iso}}}
is only one of the algebraic constraints imposed on the matrix form of
{\mathsf{T}}
by the
\G
-equivariance condition. The next section describes the further structural constraints on each block.

J.1.3
Structure of Operators between Isotypic Subspaces

In this section, we shift the focus from the input and output function spaces,

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

; and the operator
{\mathsf{T}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, to their individual isotypic subspaces,

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

for
{k}\in[1,{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
, and the operators
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
(
Equation

62
).

Recall from
Theorem

H.17
, that each isotypic subspace possesses unitary group representations that decompose into direct sums of (infinitely many) multiplicities of the irreducible representation of type
{k}
; that is:

\small\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}(g)\sim\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{\infty}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}(g)\hskip 18.49988pt\text{and}\hskip 18.49988pt\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}(g)\sim\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{\infty}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}(g).

(67)

This implies that each isotypic subspace further decomposes into (infinitely many) finite-dimensional
\G
-stable subspaces:

\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{\infty}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{k},{p}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{\infty}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{k},{p}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
. Each subspace

\mathcal{L}^{2{k},{p}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

(and similarly

\mathcal{L}^{2{k},{p}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

) has finite dimension
{d_{{k}}}\leq\infty
and its elements transform according to the irreducible representation
\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
of the group
\G
.

The modular structure of the isotypic subspaces results in a similar structure of
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
as it decomposes into
\G
-equivariant maps between the multiplicities of finite-dimensional, irreducible,
\G
-stable subspaces

{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{k},i}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{k},j}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}

for
i,j\in\mathbb{N}
, such that, in such basis the infinite-dimensional matrix form of
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
can be expressed as:

\small{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\begin{bmatrix}{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1,1}&{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1,2}&\cdots\\
{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{2,1}&\ddots&\cdots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{bmatrix}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ s.t.}\hskip 9.24994pt{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\in\text{End}_{\G}(\bar{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{k}}),\forall\;i,j\in\mathbb{N},

(68)

Where each
{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}
is constrained to be an (real) endomorphism of the vector space
\bar{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{k}}
associated to the irreducible representation
\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}:\G\to\mathbb{U}(\bar{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{k}})
.

In practical terms, this implies that each operator
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
is constructed from a combination of infinitetly many elements of the endomorphism space
\text{End}_{\G}(\bar{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{k}})
(
Definition

H.5
). Crucially, for compact symmetry groups and real irreducible representations, the space of endomorphisms is
1
,
2
or
4
dimensional, and possess an analytical basis set, defined by the map
\Psi_{{k}}:{\mathbb{K}}\to\text{End}_{\G}(\bar{{\mathcal{V}}}_{{k}})
, where
{\mathbb{K}}\in\{\R,\mathbb{C},{\mathbb{H}}\}
denotes the basis algebra associated to the irreducible representation
\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
(see details in
Proposition

H.14
).

Such a constraint implies that each
{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}
can be expressed as a linear combination of endomorphism basis elements:
{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}=\sum_{b\in{\mathbb{K}}}\alpha_{b,i,j}\Psi_{{k}}(b)
. Since every element of the endormorphism basis
\{\Psi_{{k}}(b)\}_{b\in{\mathbb{K}}}
is a finite-dimensional orthogonal matrix, this structure can be interpreted as a constraint on the dimensionality of the singular spaces of the operator
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
to be of dimension larger than
{d_{{k}}}=|\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}|
, as summarized in the following proposition:

Proposition J.3

(Minimum dimensionality of singular space of
\G
-equivariant operators between isotypic subspaces)
.

Let
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be a
\G
-equivariant operator between isotypic subspaces

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

of type
{k}
. Then,
the minimum dimension of a singular space of the operator is
{d_{{k}}}
.

Proof.

The proof follows naturally from
Proposition

H.11
, which characterizes the singular spaces of each irrep endomorphism.
∎

J.2
Finite-Rank Approximation of
\G
-Equivariant Operators

In practical applications, infinite-dimensional operators are approximated by finite-dimensional ones to enable computation. For any linear integral operator
{\mathsf{T}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, the optimal rank-
r
approximation in the Hilbert-Schmidt norm is obtained by truncating its
SVD
to the top
r
singular values and associated left/right singular functions. Let
\{\sigma_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be the singular values of
{\mathsf{T}}
in decreasing order and let
\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
,
\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be the corresponding singular functions satisfying
{\langle v_{i},{\mathsf{T}}u_{i}\rangle}_{\muy}=\sigma_{i}
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
and
{\langle v_{i},{\mathsf{T}}u_{j}\rangle}_{\muy}=0
when
i\neq j
. The best rank-
r
approximation of
{\mathsf{T}}
is then given by
(
Eckart & Young, 1936
)
:

\small{\mathsf{T}}_{r}f=\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}{\langle u_{i},f\rangle}_{\mux}v_{i},\hskip 9.24994pt\forall f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},\hskip 18.49988pt\iff\hskip 18.49988pt\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\approx\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}u_{i}({\bm{x}})v_{i}({\bm{y}}).

(69)

Since the left and right singular functions form orthonormal bases for

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

, a rank-
r
approximation reduces these infinite-dimensional spaces to the
r
-dimensional subspaces
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}=\text{span}(\{u_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
and
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}=\text{span}(\{v_{i}\}_{i=1}^{r})
.

When

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

are symmetric function spaces with group actions
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}
and
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}
of a compact group
\G
, and
{\mathsf{T}}
is
\G
-equivariant, the finite-rank approximation
{\mathsf{T}}_{r}:\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}\to\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}
must satisfy that for all
f\in\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}
,
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}
, and
g\in\G
, both
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}f\in\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}
and
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}h\in\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}
. This ensures that
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}[{\mathsf{T}}_{r}f]={\mathsf{T}}_{r}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}f]
(see
Appendix

I
).

Moreover, since

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

decompose orthogonally into isotypic subspaces,
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}=\bm{\oplus}^{\perp}_{1\leq{k}\leq{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}=\bm{\oplus}^{\perp}_{1\leq{k}\leq{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, the operator
{\mathsf{T}}
is completely determined by the
{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}
operators
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
(see
Section

J.1.2
). Thus, the
\G
-equivariance of
{\mathsf{T}}_{r}
depends on that of each finite-rank operator
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{r_{{k}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\to\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
, which requires the approximated subspaces
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
to be
\G
-stable. For simplicity, we assume
|\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}|=|\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}|=r_{{k}}
, although this equality need not hold in general.

Constraints on the Dimensionality of Truncation

Each approximation of an isotypic subspace

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

(and similarly

\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

) is
\G
-stable if the group representation is defined using a truncated multiplicity
m_{{k}}<\infty
for the
{k}^{\text{th}}
irreducible representation, i.e.
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}$}}\sim\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{m_{{k}}}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
and
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}\sim\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{m_{{k}}}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
. Consequently, the dimension of the approximated subspaces is multiple of the irreducible representation’s dimension:
r_{{k}}={d_{{k}}}\,m_{{k}}
(see
Section

J.1.3
).

Spectral Decomposition

Given the block-diagonal structure of the operator
{\mathsf{T}}
in the isotypic basis (
Equation

65
), the truncated
SVD
of
{\mathsf{T}}
reduces to performing the truncated
SVD
of each per-isotypic operator
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
. Let
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be the
\G
-stable finite-dimensional approximations of the input/output spaces of
{\mathsf{T}}
, endowed with group representations

\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}$}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}$}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{m_{{k}}}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}

and

\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}$}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}$}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\bm{\oplus}_{{p}=1}^{m_{{k}}}\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
. Here,
m_{{k}}\in\mathbb{N}
denotes the multiplicity of the irreducible representation of type
{k}
, and
d_{{k}}:=|\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}|
is its dimension. Then, the structural constraints on the
SVD
of the restriction of
{\mathsf{T}}
to these spaces are summarized in the following theorem:

Theorem J.4

(Isotypic-spectral basis)
.

Let
{\mathsf{T}}
be a
\G
-equivariant operator and let
{\mathsf{T}}_{\star}\colon\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}\to\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}
be its
\G
-equivariant restriction in finite dimensions. Then, the singular value decomposition of the restricted operator matrix representation
{\bm{T}}_{\star}
reduces to:

\displaystyle\small{\bm{T}}_{\star}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\bm{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\star}

\displaystyle=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\bm{W}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}_{\star}{\bm{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}_{\star}{\bm{M}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\top}_{\star}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\bm{U}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\star}({\bm{\Sigma}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\star}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}){\bm{V}}_{\star}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\top}

Where
{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}
denotes the identity matrix in
d_{{k}}
-dimensions and

{\bm{\Sigma}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\star}
denotes the infinite-dimensional diagonal matrix singular values per irreducible
\G
-stable subspace.

Thm.

J.4
shows that symmetries force each isotypic subspace’s singular space to have dimension at least
d_{{k}}
, which is the minimum required for a faithful representation of
\G^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
(see
Definition

H.9
). Because in practice our goal is to approximate the top
r
singular spaces of
{\mathsf{T}}
, this result precisely characterizes the constraints imposed by
\G
-equivariance on the optimal rank-
r
truncation’s spectral basis and corresponding kernel function in
Equation

13
, as summarized in the following corollary:

Corollary J.5

(Symmetry constraints on the spectral basis)
.

Let
{\mathsf{T}}
be a
\G
-equivariant operator and let
{\mathsf{T}}_{\star}\colon\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}$}}\to\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}
be its
\G
-equivariant restriction in
r
-dimensions. Then, the spectral basis of
{\mathsf{T}}_{\star}
is given by:

\small\pmd_{\star}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\pmd^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\star}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\sum_{s=1}^{r_{k}}\sigma^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}_{s}\sum_{i=1}^{d_{{k}}}u_{s,i}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}({\bm{x}})v_{s,i}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}({\bm{y}}),

(70)

where
\{u_{s,i}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\}_{i\in[d_{{k}}]}
and
\{v_{s,i}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\}_{i\in[d_{{k}}]}
are the left and right singular basis sets of the
s^{\text{th}}
singular space of
{\mathsf{T}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
. Note that the truncated dimension is restricted by the dimensionality and multiplicities of the individual irreducible representations
r=\sum_{{k}=1}^{d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}r_{k}=\sum_{{k}=1}^{d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}d_{{k}}m_{k}
.

Appendix K
Relevant
\G
-Equivariant Operators in Probability Theory

In this section we study the properties of expectations and covariances of functions of symmetric random variables in the presence our assumed symmetry priors
Equation

6
. In a nutshell, we characterize how expectations of observables of symmetric random variables are invariant to the group action, and that the covariance and cross-covariance matrices in these spaces are
\G
-equivariant and hence inherit rich structural constraints that can aid in empirical estimation.

Let
({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})
be two vector-valued random variables over the probability spaces
({\mathcal{X}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{X}}},\mux)
and
({\mathcal{Y}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\muy)
, with

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

being the corresponding square-integrable function spaces and
\mathds{1}_{\mux}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
,
\mathds{1}_{\muy}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
the characteristic functions of sets with nonzero probability.

When

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

are symmetric function spaces (see
Appendix

I
), denote their orthogonal isotypic decompositions by
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}:=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
(cf.
Theorem

H.17
). Any function
f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
or
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
decomposes as
f=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
and
h=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}h^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
(see
Equation

63
). By convention, the first isotypic subspace corresponds to the trivial group action. Thus, we write
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{\text{inv}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}:=\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{21}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\subset\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and denote the
\G
-invariant component of
f
by
f^{{\text{inv}}}:=f^{(1)}
(and similarly for

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

).

K.1
The Expectation Operator

The expected value of a function
f\in\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}:=\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
can be interpreted as the result of applying a linear integral operator that projects each
f\in\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}
to a constant function evaluating to the function’s expected value
\mathbb{E}_{\mux}f
.

Definition K.1

(Expectation operator)
.

Let
\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}\subseteq\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
be a function space. The expectation operator
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}:\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}\mapsto\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}
is a linear integral operator defined by a constant kernel function
k_{\mathbb{E}}({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}^{\prime})=\mathds{1}_{P_{{\mathbf{x}}}}({\bm{x}})\mathds{1}_{P_{{\mathbf{x}}}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})
for all
{\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{X}}
, such that this operator maps any function
f
to a constant function that evaluates to the function’s expected value
\mathds{1}_{P_{{\mathbf{x}}}}(\cdot)\mathbb{E}_{\mux}f
, that is:

\small[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}f]({\bm{x}}^{\prime})=\int_{{\mathcal{X}}}k_{\mathbb{E}}({\bm{x}},{\bm{x}}^{\prime})f({\bm{x}})\mu(d{\bm{x}})=\mathds{1}_{P_{{\mathbf{x}}}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\int_{{\mathcal{X}}}f({\bm{x}})\mu(d{\bm{x}})\equiv\mathds{1}_{P_{{\mathbf{x}}}}({\bm{x}}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{\mux}f.

(71)

Whenever

{\mathcal{F}}

is a symmetric function space, the operator
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}
commutes with the group action and is
\G
-invariant (
Definition

H.12
):

Proposition K.2

(
\G
-invariant expectation operator)
.

Let

{\mathcal{F}}

be a symmetric function space with the action
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}$}}}}
of a compact symmetry group
\G
. Then, the expectation operator commutes with the group action and is a
\G
-invariant operator
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}:\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}\mapsto\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{\text{inv}}\subseteq\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}
:

\small{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}$}}}}f]=g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}$}}}}[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}f]\hskip 18.49988pt\text{and}\hskip 18.49988pt{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}f={\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}$}}}}f]\in\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{\text{inv}},\hskip 18.49988pt\forall\;f\in\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$},g\in\G.

(72)

Proof.

The operator
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}
commutes with the group action as its kernel function
k_{\mathbb{E}}
is constant and therefore
\G
-invariant (
Definition

J.1
). Furthermore since the image of the expectation operator are constant functions, these functions belong to the subspace of
\G
-invariant functions,
\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{\text{inv}}
.
∎

As an operator that commutes with the group action, the expectation operator decomposes into
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}:=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
, where
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}:\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\mapsto\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
denotes the restriction of
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}
to the isotypic subspace
\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
(
Section

J.1.2
). However, since the image of the operator lies in the subspace of
\G
-invariant functions,
\Im({\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}})\subset\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}^{\text{inv}}
, it follows that
{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\bm{0}
for every
{k}\neq{\text{inv}}
. Consequently, we obtain the following:

Corollary K.3

(Expectation of a function depends only on its
\G
-invariant component)
.

For any function
f\in\scalebox{0.9}{${\mathcal{F}}$}
, the expectation depends only on its
\G
-invariant component:

[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}f](\cdot)=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}](\cdot)=[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{\text{inv}}}f^{{\text{inv}}}](\cdot):=\mathds{1}_{\mu}(\cdot)\mathbb{E}_{\mu}f^{{\text{inv}}}.

(73)

Corollary K.4

(Functions without a
\G
-invariant component are centered)
.

Any function
f=\sum_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
without a
\G
-invariant compoment, i.e.,
f^{{\text{inv}}}=0
, is centered:

[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}f](\cdot)=\sum_{{k}=2}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}[{\mathsf{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}](\cdot)=\mathds{1}_{\mu}(\cdot)0,\qquad\iff\qquad\mathbb{E}_{\mu}f=0,\quad\forall\;f\in{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2{\text{inv}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}}^{\perp}.

(74)

To better comprehend these concepts we refer the reader to
Example

I.4
.

K.2
The Cross-Covariance Operator

Given two vector-valued random variables
({\mathbf{x}}=[{\textnormal{x}}_{1},\dots,{\textnormal{x}}_{n}],{\mathbf{y}}=[{\textnormal{y}}_{1},\dots,{\textnormal{y}}_{m}])
defined on the measure spaces
({\mathcal{X}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{X}}},\mux)
and
({\mathcal{Y}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\muy)
, a key statistic assessing the linear relationship between scalar components is the covariance:

\small\mathrm{Cov}({\textnormal{x}}_{i},{\textnormal{y}}_{j})=\mathbb{E}_{\muxy}[({\textnormal{x}}_{i}-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{\textnormal{x}}_{i}])({\textnormal{y}}_{j}-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\textnormal{y}}_{j}])]=\mathbb{E}_{\muxy}[{\textnormal{x}}_{i}{\textnormal{y}}_{j}]-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{\textnormal{x}}_{i}]\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\textnormal{y}}_{j}].

For vector-valued random variables, the cross-covariance matrix
\mathrm{Cov}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})\in\R^{n\times m}
is defined entrywise by
\mathrm{Cov}({\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}})_{i,j}:=\mathrm{Cov}({\textnormal{x}}_{i},{\textnormal{y}}_{j})
. The cross-covariance operator is the extension of this concept to the Hilbert spaces of functions

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

.

Definition K.5

(Cross-covariance operator
(
Fukumizu et al., 2004
)
)
.

Let
\scalebox{0.85}{${\mathcal{F}}$}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{x}}$}}\subseteq\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\subseteq\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be two Hilbert spaces of functions defined on the random variables
{\mathbf{x}}
and
{\mathbf{y}}
, which take values in the measure spaces
({\mathcal{X}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{X}}},\mux)
and
({\mathcal{Y}},\Sigma_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\muy)
, respectively. The cross-covariance operator
{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
is a linear integral operator defined by

\small{\langle f,{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}h\rangle}_{\mux}:=\mathrm{Cov}(f,h)=\mathbb{E}_{\muxy}[f({\mathbf{x}})h({\mathbf{y}})]-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[f({\mathbf{x}})]\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[h({\mathbf{y}})],\hskip 18.49988pt\forall\;f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{},\;h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}.

(75)

Choosing separable basis sets for the two spaces,
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}=\{\phi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}=\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, the matrix representation of the cross-covariance operator has entries

[{\bm{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}}]_{i,j}:={\langle\phi_{i},{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}\psi_{j}\rangle}_{\mux}=\mathrm{Cov}(\phi_{i},\psi_{j}),

where the covariance is computed with respect to the joint measure
\muxy
and the marginals
\mux
and
\muy
. Given a dataset of
N
samples from the joint distribution
({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})\sim\muxy
, the empirical estimate of the matrix form of the cross-covariance operator is

\small\widehat{{\bm{C}}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{n=1}^{N}\bm{\phi}({\bm{x}}_{n})\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}}_{n})^{\top}-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{x}}[\bm{\phi}({\bm{x}}_{n})]\,\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathbf{y}}[\bm{\psi}({\bm{y}}_{n})]^{\top},\hskip 9.24994pt\bm{\phi}(\cdot)=[\phi(\cdot)]_{i\in\mathbb{N}},\;\bm{\psi}(\cdot)=[\psi(\cdot)]_{i\in\mathbb{N}}.

(76)

Note that the adjoint of the operator is defined by
{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}^{*}={\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{y}}{\mathbf{x}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
. In the case
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}=\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
, the cross‐covariance operator reduces to the covariance operator, and has an analog definition to
Definition

K.5
.

Covariance and Cross-Covariance Operators of Symmetric Hilbert Spaces of Functions

Whenever

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

are symmetric function spaces, and the joint probability measure is
\G
-invaraint, the cross-covariance operator
{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}
commute with the group action and is
\G
-equivariant (
Section

H.1
):

Proposition K.6

(
\G
-equivariant cross-covariance operator)
.

Let
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\subseteq\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\subseteq\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
be symmetric Hilbert spaces of functions endowed with the group actions
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}
and
\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}
of a compact symmetry group
\G
. Then, whenever the joint probability measure is
\G
-invariant, i.e.,
\muxy({\mathbb{B}},{\mathbb{A}})=\muxy(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{B}},g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}})
for all
g\in\G,{\mathbb{B}}\in\Sigma_{\mathcal{X}},{\mathbb{A}}\in\Sigma_{Y}
, the cross-covariance operator
{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}:\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
(
Definition

K.5
) commutes with the group actions and is a
\G
-equivariant operator (
Definition

J.1
):

\small g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}[{\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}h]={\mathsf{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}h],\hskip 18.49988pt\forall\;h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{},g\in\G.

(77)

Proof.

To proof that the operator is
\G
-equivariant we must show its kernel function is
\G
-invariant (see
Definition

J.1
). The proof follows naturally in any regular basis of the input and output functions spaces
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}=\{\phi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}=\{\psi_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
, in which the group action on basis functions acts by permutations of basis functions, such that,
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{i}\equiv\phi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i}\in{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}
and
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}}}\psi_{j}\equiv\psi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}\in{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}
, where
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i,g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j\in\mathbb{N}
. Then we must show that that:

\displaystyle k({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\pmd(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\bm{x}},g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\bm{y}})\hskip 18.49988pt

\displaystyle\forall\;g\in\G,{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{X}},{\bm{y}}\in{\mathcal{Y}}

(78)

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}[{\bm{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}}]_{i,j}\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\psi_{j}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}[{\bm{C}}_{{\mathbf{x}},{\mathbf{y}}}]_{i,j}[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{i}]({\bm{x}})[g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}\psi_{j}]({\bm{y}})\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle\text{s.t. \lx@cref{creftypeplural~refnum}{def:action_funct_space} and\lx@nobreakspace\lx@cref{refnum}{def:cross_covariance_operator}}

\displaystyle\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\mathrm{Cov}(\phi_{i},\psi_{j})\phi_{i}({\bm{x}})\psi_{j}({\bm{y}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}}\mathrm{Cov}(\phi_{i},\psi_{j})\phi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}i}({\bm{x}})\psi_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}j}({\bm{y}}).\hskip 9.24994pt

Hence, the cross-covariance operator’s kernel function is
\G
-invariant only if the covariance is
\G
-invariant:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Cov}(\phi_{i},\psi_{j})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}}}\phi_{i},g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}\psi_{j})

\displaystyle\forall\;g\in\G,i,j\in\mathbb{N}

(79)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\muxy}[\phi_{i}({\mathbf{x}})\psi_{j}({\mathbf{y}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\muxy}[\phi_{i}(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbf{x}})\psi_{j}(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})]\hskip 9.24994pt

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mu}f=\mathbb{E}_{\mu}g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}f

\displaystyle\int_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}\phi_{i}({\mathbf{x}})\psi_{j}({\mathbf{y}})\,\muxy(d{\mathbf{x}},d{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}\phi_{i}(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbf{x}})\psi_{j}(g^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})\,\muxy(d{\mathbf{x}},d{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\int_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}\phi_{i}({\mathbf{x}})\psi_{j}({\mathbf{y}})\,\muxy(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}d{\mathbf{x}},g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}d{\mathbf{y}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Cov}(\phi_{i},\psi_{j}).

∎

An equivalent result follows for covariance operators of symmetric Hilbert spaces.

Appendix L
Statistical Learning Theory

This section provides the development and proofs of the statistical learning guarantees in
Theorem

5.1
for regression and conditional probability estimation using our proposed model.

Recall that regression and conditional probabilities can be expressed in terms of the conditional expectation operator
\CE\colon\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
(see
Equations

1
and
2
). Given that the operator is compact
(
Kostic et al., 2024b
)
, it admits a singular value decomposition. Hence, the kernel function defining the operator
Equation

1
can be expanded in terms of the operator spectral basis:

\small\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}}):=\frac{dP_{{\mathbf{x}}{\mathbf{y}}}({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})}{d(\mux({\bm{x}})\times\muy({\bm{y}}))}=\sum_{i=0}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}u_{i}({\bm{x}})v_{i}({\bm{y}}).

(80)

Where
(\sigma_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
denotes the operator’s singular values, and
(u_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
and
(v_{i})_{i\in\mathbb{N}}
denote the left and right singular functions, which form complete orthonormal basis sets for

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}

and

\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}

, respectively.
Given that the operator’s first singular value is
\sigma_{0}=1
, associated with the constant functions
u_{0}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}}
,
v_{0}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}
, the conditional expectation operator can be defined as:

\CE=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}u_{i}{\langle v_{i},\cdot\rangle}_{\muy}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}}{\langle\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{Y}}},\cdot\rangle}_{\muy}+\underbrace{\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sigma_{i}u_{i}{\langle v_{i},\cdot\rangle}_{\muy}}_{{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}}.

(81)

Where
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
denotes the
deflated
operator, excluding the first eigen triplet
(\sigma_{0},u_{0},v_{0})
. Leveraging the SVD of
\CE
, we approximate the operator’s action for any
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
using a rank-
r
(
1<r<\infty
) operator given by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[h({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!=\!{\bm{x}}]

\displaystyle=[\CE h]({\bm{x}})\approx\mathbb{E}[h({\mathbf{y}})]+\sum_{i=1}^{r}\sigma_{i}u^{\nnParams}_{i}({\bm{x}})\mathbb{E}[v^{\nnParams}_{i}({\mathbf{y}})h({\mathbf{y}})],

(82)

\displaystyle\text{s.t.}\;\mathbb{E}[u^{\nnParams}_{i}({\mathbf{x}})]=\mathbb{E}[v^{\nnParams}_{i}({\mathbf{y}})]=0,\forall\;i\geq 1.

Where
(u^{\nnParams}_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
and
(v^{\nnParams}_{i})_{i=1}^{r}
denote parametrizations of the top-
r
left and right singular functions. Given that the operator’s kernel
Equation

80
preserves the probability mass, that is
\int_{{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}}\pmd({\bm{x}},{\bm{y}})d\mux({\bm{x}})d\muy({\bm{y}})=1
, every non-constant singular function is constrained to be centered, as described in the r.h.s of
Equation

82
.

In the context of symmetries, we note that
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
admits a block-diagonal structure w.r.t. to isotypic basis of associated

\mathcal{L}^{2}

spaces. Indeed we have the following from
Theorem

J.4
.

{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{*}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}*}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}=1}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\big[({\mathsf{U}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}{\mathsf{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}{\mathsf{V}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}*})\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big].

(83)

Where the unitary operators
{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\colon\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}\colon\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
change the basis to the isotypic decompositions
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}$}}=\{\phi^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\}_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],\,i\in[m_{k}],\,j\in[d_{{k}}]}
and
{\mathbb{I}}_{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}$}}=\{\psi^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\}_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],\,i\in[m_{k}],\,j\in[d_{{k}}]}
, with
i
indexing each irreducible
\G
-stable subspace and
j
indexing the dimensions within that subspace (see
Section

J.2
).

Further, by
Theorem

J.4
, the
SVD
of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
forces each isotypic subspace to have dimension at least
d_{{k}}=\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${k}$}}
for every
{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
.

{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}*}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\big[{\mathsf{U}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]\big[{\mathsf{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]\big[{\mathsf{V}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]^{*},\,{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],

(84)

where
{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}*}
and
{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}*}
are orthogonal projectors on
{k}
-th isotypic subspace, and

{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}^{*}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}{\mathsf{Q}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}=\big[{\bm{I}}_{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\otimes{\mathsf{U}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]\big[I_{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\otimes{\mathsf{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]\big[I_{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}\otimes{\mathsf{V}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}\big]^{*}.

(85)

Further, observe that the singular values of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
are elements of positive diagonal operators
{\mathsf{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}
, denoted as
({\mathsf{S}}^{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}})_{i}=\sigma_{i}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
,
while the left and right singular functions are
u^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}
and
v^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}
, respectively, for
i\in\mathbb{N}
,
j\in[d_{{k}}]
and
{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
, where
{\bm{e}}^{d}_{j}
is
j
-th vector of standard basis of
\R^{d}
.

Given the constraints on the spectral basis of
\G
-equivariant operators (see
Corollary

J.5
), our representation learning procedure approach results in feature maps:

\displaystyle\small{\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}[{\bm{e}}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}_{{k}}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{m}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}]\,u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}(\cdot)\colon{\mathcal{X}}\to\R^{r_{m}}

(86)

\displaystyle{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}(\cdot)

\displaystyle=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}[{\bm{e}}^{{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}}_{{k}}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{m}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}]\,v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}(\cdot)\colon{\mathcal{X}}\to\R^{r_{m}},

which can further be separated into
{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}
orthogonal blocks
{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}=\sum_{i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}\phi^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}
and
\bm{\psi}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=\sum_{i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}\psi^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}
as

{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}=\sum_{i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}[{\bm{e}}^{m}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}]\,u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}(\cdot)\quad\text{ and }\quad{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}=\sum_{i\in[m],j\in[d_{{k}}]}[{\bm{e}}^{m}_{i}\otimes{\bm{e}}^{d_{{k}}}_{j}]\,v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}(\cdot).

(87)

In addition, the singular value matrices have a tensor form

{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}=diag({\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{(1)},\ldots,{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{({n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}})})
,
where
{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}=diag(\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1},\ldots,\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{m})\otimes{\bm{I}}_{d_{{k}}}

and

\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}\in[0,1]\;,i\in[m],{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
.
Thus, we obtain the operator
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}={\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}-\mathds{1}_{\mux}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\muy}
in block form,

{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}=\bm{\oplus}_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
, where each
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}
acts on the
{k}
-th isotypic subspace as

[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}f]({\bm{x}}):={{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\,\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\mathbf{y}})f^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})],\quad f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{},

(88)

and hence

[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}f]({\bm{x}}):={\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}\,\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}})f({\mathbf{y}})],\quad f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}.

(89)

Finally, we extend the definition of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
to vector-valued observables
{\bm{h}}\colon{\mathcal{Y}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
via basis expansions.

[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}}):=\sum_{\ell}{\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}\,\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}})({\langle{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}}),{\bm{z}}_{\ell}\rangle}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{z}}_{\ell})],\quad{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})

(90)

where
({\bm{z}}_{i})_{i\in[n_{{\mathcal{Z}}}]}
is the orthonormal basis of
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

By doing so, we ensure that
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
and, consequently,
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
are
\G
-equivariant operators for both the scalar map
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
and the vector-valued map
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})
. Moreover, a direct consequence of (
90
) is as follows.

Proposition L.1
.

Let with
{\mathcal{Z}}
being a real Euclidean space endowed with symmetry group
\G
, and let
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\colon\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\mapsto\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})
be given by
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{y}})]+{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}
. Then for every
\G
-equivariant
{\bm{f}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})

and every
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{X}}

\small[{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}](g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\bm{x}})=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{y}})]+[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}](g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\bm{x}})=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{y}})]+g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}}}[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}]({\bm{x}})=g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}}}[{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{f}}]({\bm{x}}).

(91)

Proof.

Since
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
is
\G
-equivaraint, for every
g\in\G
we have that

\small[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}](g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}{\bm{x}})=[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}(g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}\cdot)]]({\bm{x}})=\sum_{i}{\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}\,\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}}){\langle{\bm{h}}(g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{Y}}}{\mathbf{y}}),{\bm{z}}_{i}\rangle}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}],

which, using
g
instead of
g^{{-}1}
and the assumption that
f
is
\G
-equivariant, implies

\displaystyle(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}{\bm{x}})

\displaystyle=\!\sum_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}}){\langle g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}}),{\bm{z}}_{i}\rangle}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}]

\displaystyle=\!\sum_{i}({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}\,\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{v}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}}){\langle{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}}),g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{z}}_{i}\rangle}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}){\bm{z}}_{i}.

Thus, changing the basis to
(g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\bm{z}}_{i})_{i\in[n_{\mathcal{Z}}]}
we obtain the result when
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]=0
. But since
\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}_{{\mathcal{X}}}{\bm{x}})=1
for every
{\bm{x}}\in{\mathcal{X}}
and
g\in\G
, the same holds for
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
.
∎

Recall that for the effective latent dimension
m
the true latent dimension is constrained by the dimensionality of the singular spaces, i.e.,
{r_{m}}=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}r_{k}=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}m[{k}]d_{{k}}
.
Further, given a measurable set
{\mathbb{A}}\subseteq{\mathcal{X}}
and collection of group elements
\G^{\prime}\subseteq\G
, let us define the following symmetry index of a set
{\mathbb{A}}
w.r.t. probability distribution of random variable
{\mathbf{x}}

\small\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|(|\G^{\prime}|-1)}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g_{1},g_{2}\in\G^{\prime}\\
g_{1}\neq g_{2}\end{subarray}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in g_{1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap g_{2}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]},

(92)

which in the case when
\G^{\prime}
is a subgroup of
\G
simplifies as

\small\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\G^{\prime}\\
g\neq e\end{subarray}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}\cap g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}.

(93)

Observe that always
\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})\in[0,1]
, where extremes correspond to the cases
\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})=1
when set
{\mathbb{A}}
is
\G^{\prime}
invariant, and
\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})=0
when
{\mathbb{A}}
equals its coset w.r.t.
\G^{\prime}
, that is
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap{\mathbb{A}}=\emptyset
for all
g\in\G^{\prime}
, meaning that the set is fully asymmetric w.r.t transformations
g\in\G^{\prime}
.

We first generalize the approximation error bound in Lemma 1 from
(
Kostic et al., 2024b
)
to the case of vector valued functions in the presence of symmetries.

Theorem L.2

(Approximation error)
.

Given a group of symmetries
\G
, let
{\mathcal{X}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}
be Hilbert spaces endowed with symmetry group
\G
, and let
\mux
,
\muy
and
\muxy
be
\G
-invariant probability distributions on
{\mathcal{X}}
,
{\mathcal{Y}}
and
{\mathcal{X}}\times{\mathcal{Y}}
. Then, for every
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
it holds that

\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}=\cdot]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\leq\left(\sigma_{{r_{m}}+1}^{\star}+\left\|[\![{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}]\!]_{r_{m}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|\right)\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}.

(94)

Moreover, denoting

{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[f({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[f]+\frac{\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}f]({\mathbf{x}})]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]},

(95)

if
{\bm{h}}
is either
\G^{\prime}
-invariant or
\G^{\prime}
-equivariant for some
\G^{\prime}\subseteq\G
, then for every measurable set
{\mathbb{A}}

\small\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]{-}{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}{\leq}\left(\sigma_{{r_{m}}+1}^{\star}+\left\|[\![{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}]\!]_{r_{m}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|\right)\tfrac{\left\|h\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}\sqrt{\tfrac{1+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})}{|\G^{\prime}|}}.

(96)

Proof.

Start by observing that

\displaystyle\small\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}=\cdot]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}({\mathcal{Y}})\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}})}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})},

where the equality holds since we extended operators
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
and
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
to vector valued setting as integral operators with the same scalar kernel. Hence, (
94
) readily follows.

To prove (
96
), start with noting

\small\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}.

Then, if
{\bm{h}}
is
\G
-equivariant, then, using that invariance of the probability distribution
\mux
,
\G
-equivariance of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}
and, due to Proposition
L.1
, of
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
, we have that for every
g\in\G^{\prime}\subseteq\G

\displaystyle\small\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbf{x}})[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}](g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbf{x}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\,g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}}}[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}(g)\,[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})].

Hence, averaging over
\G^{\prime}
we obtain

\small\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}})\overline{{\bm{z}}}({\mathbf{x}})],

where

\small{\mathsf{H}}({\bm{x}})=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\bm{x}})\bar{\bm{\rho}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}(g)\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ and }\hskip 9.24994pt{\bm{z}}({\bm{x}})=[({\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}){\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}}).

Since due to Cauchy-Schwartz inequality we have

\small\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}}){\bm{z}}({\mathbf{x}})]\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq[\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\left\|{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}}][\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\left\|{\bm{z}}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}]=\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}\,[\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\left\|{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}}]

and
\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\leq\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})\to\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}
, it remains to bound
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\left\|{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}}
. But, the group actions in the vector spaces are unitary, so using the
\G
-invariance of the distribution of
{\mathbf{x}}
we obtain

\displaystyle\small\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\left\|{\mathsf{H}}({\mathbf{x}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}\to{\mathcal{Z}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}\Big[\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\bm{x}})\Big]^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|^{2}{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]^{2}}\sum_{g,g^{\prime}\in\G^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\bm{x}})\mathds{1}_{g^{\prime-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\bm{x}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|^{2}{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]^{2}}\sum_{g,g^{\prime}\in\G^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap g^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\bm{x}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|^{2}{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\sum_{g,g^{\prime}\in\G^{\prime}}\frac{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap g^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\frac{1+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})}{|\G^{\prime}|},
