which completes the proof of (
96
) for
\G^{\prime}
-equivariant functions. Finally, if
f
is
\G^{\prime}
-invariant, the proof follows the same lines by replacing group actions
(\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}}})
by their respective group representation
\bm{\rho}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Z}}$}}
(see
Definition

H.4
) with identity.
∎

Next we analyze the errors when, instead of applying learned operators
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
, we apply their empirical counterparts in inference tasks. To that end, we define now estimators of
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{x}})]
and
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{z}}({\mathbf{y}})]
exploiting the
\G
-invariance of the distributions of
{\mathbf{x}}
and
{\mathbf{y}}
. First, define the empirical
\G
-invariant distributions

\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}:=\frac{1}{|\G|N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{g\in g}\delta_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbf{x}}_{i}}(\cdot),\quad\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}:=\frac{1}{|\G|N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{g\in g}\delta_{g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbf{y}}_{i}}(\cdot).

Hence we can define the equivariant empirical mean of any function
f\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}
,
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}
as

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[f]=\frac{1}{|\G|N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{g\in\G}f(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbf{x}}_{i}),\quad\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[h]=\frac{1}{|\G|N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{g\in\G}h(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}}_{i}).

(97)

This extends naturally to operator on a function space
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
where
{\mathcal{Z}}
is endowed with an inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
. If the distribution of
{\mathbf{y}}
is
\G^{\prime}
-invariant, then for any
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
, we use the estimator
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]
in (
97
) as an estimator of
\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]
:

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]=\frac{1}{|\G|N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{g\in\G}{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}}_{i}).

(98)

In this notation, we define our empirical estimators

\small[{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}})\approx[\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}})=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\sum_{i\in[m]}\sum_{j\in[d_{{k}}]}\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}({\bm{x}})\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}{\bm{h}}]

and

\small{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\!\approx\!\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\!=\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\sum_{i\in[m]}\sum_{j\in[d_{{k}}]}\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}{\bm{h}}].

and, by choosing
{\bm{h}}=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{B}}
,

\small P[{\mathbf{y}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]\approx\widehat{P}_{\nnParams}[{\mathbf{y}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]\!=\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{B}}]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\sum_{i\in[m]}\sum_{j\in[d_{{k}}]}\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i}\frac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{i,j}\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{B}}].

Direct consequence of the above construction which ensures that
\widehat{P}_{{\mathbf{x}}}
and
\widehat{P}_{{\mathbf{y}}}
are
\G
-invariant is the following result.

Proposition L.3
.

Let
\mux
and
\muy
are
\G
-invariant, and
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
from (
89
) is
\G
-equivariant model, and let
z\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}({\mathcal{X}},\R)
and
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
be arbitrary. If for every
{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]

\small\Big\{\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\right\|,\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}}){{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})^{\top}]-{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\|,\left\|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}}){{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})^{\top}]-{\bm{I}}_{m}\right\|\Big\}\leq\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}

holds with
{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}=[u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1,1}|\ldots|u^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{m,1}]^{\top}\in\R^{m}

and

{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}=[v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1,1}|\ldots|v^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{m,1}]^{\top}\in\R^{m}
, and if

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})]\right\|}{\left\|z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}}}\leq A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},z),

(99)

\displaystyle\frac{\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}\otimes{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})\otimes{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})]\right\|}{\left\|{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}}}\leq A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}}),

where
z=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\sum_{j\in[d_{{k}}]}z^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{j}
and
{\bm{h}}=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\sum_{j\in[d_{{k}}]}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{j}
are isospectral decompositions, then

\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}\!-\!\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}+\Big[1+\max_{k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Big]^{3}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}\,[A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})]^{2}.

(100)

Moreover, the empirical estimation error
is upper bounded by

\displaystyle\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}\leq

(101)

\displaystyle(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})^{3}\Big[A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},z)+A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})+A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},z)A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\Big]^{2}\left\|z\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}})}^{2}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}.

Proof.

First, observe that due to
\G
-invariance of distribution
\muxy
and
\G
-equivaraince of
{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
and
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
we have that

{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}},

(102)

and

\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z^{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})]\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\mathbf{x}})].

(103)

In the same way, since the empirical distributions
\widehat{P}_{{\mathbf{x}}}
and
\widehat{P}_{{\mathbf{y}}}
are
\G
-invariant, we have that

\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}},

(104)

and

\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z[\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]]=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z^{(1)}]\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}]+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]],

(105)

where

[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{x}})={{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}].

(106)

Therefore, combining (
102
) and (
104
), we obtain that

\small\begin{split}[{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}})-[\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}})&=\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}]\Big)\mathds{1}_{{\mathcal{X}}}({\bm{x}})\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\Big([{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{x}})-[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{x}})\Big),\end{split}

which after taking the norm in
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})
, due to orthonormality of isotypic subspaces gives

\small\begin{split}\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}]\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}&=\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}^{(1)}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\left\|[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}.\end{split}

Now, observe that, since

\small[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{x}})-[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]({\bm{x}})={{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Big)

applying the norm we have that
\left\|[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}
equals

\small\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Big)^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\bm{x}}){{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}({\bm{x}})^{\top}]\Big){\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Big(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Big)

which using constraints within each isotypic block and

\small\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(m)}({\bm{x}}){{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(m)}({\bm{x}})^{\top}]\preceq\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(m)}({\bm{x}}){{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(m)}({\bm{x}})^{\top}]\right\|{\bm{I}}_{m}\leq(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}){\bm{I}}_{m},

implies, due to (
99
), that

\displaystyle\small\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}-{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d_{{k}}\,(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}})\,(\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1})^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}({\mathbf{y}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}\otimes{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}]\right\|_{\R^{m}\times{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq d_{{k}}\,(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}})\,(\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1})^{2}\,[A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})]^{2}\,\left\|{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}.

Therefore, bounding
\sigma_{1}^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\leq\sigma_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}+|\sigma_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}-\sigma_{1}^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}|\leq 1+\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\right\|
and summing over isotypic components, since
\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}],j\in[d_{{k}}]}\left\|{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{j}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}=\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}d_{{k}}\left\|{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}^{2}
, we complete the proof of (
100
).

To show (
101
), we combine (
103
) and (
105
), and obtain that
\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]
can be written as

\small\sum_{{k}\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}d_{{k}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})]^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]^{\top}{\bm{S}}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Bigg].

Adding and subtracting mixed terms we then obtain for each isotypic component,
\frac{1}{d_{{k}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}]]
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})]^{\top}{\bm{S}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Bigg)

\displaystyle\!+\!\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{1}z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Bigg)^{\top}{\bm{S}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})]

\displaystyle\!+\!\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{x}})z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{x}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{{\bm{u}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{1}z_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Bigg)^{\top}{\bm{S}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}({\mathbf{y}})\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}({\mathbf{y}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{{\bm{v}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{\nnParams}}_{(1)}\!\otimes\!{\bm{h}}_{1}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}]\Bigg),

and consequently bounded using (
99
) as

\small\begin{split}\small\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[z[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}&\leq d_{{k}}\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}\,\Big[A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},z)+A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},z)A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\Big]\left\|z^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}})}\left\|{\bm{h}}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}.\end{split}

Summing across isotypic components and bounding
\sigma^{\nnParams{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}_{1}
as before, we complete the proof.
∎

First note that coupling (
100
) with (
94
) ensures that we can prove regression bound via concentration result ensuring (
99
). To obtain similar result for set-wise regression, we set
z=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}
and use (
101
) to obtain the following.

Proposition L.4
.

Under the assumptions of Proposition
L.3
, let
A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}})A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\leq A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}})+A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})
. If

|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]|/\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]\leq\eta_{\mathbb{A}}

(107)

and
\eta_{\mathbb{A}}<1/2
, then

\small\begin{split}\small\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}&\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}+\frac{2\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{P[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\times\Big[2(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})\Big(A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}})+A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\Big)+\eta_{\mathbb{A}}\Big],\end{split}

(108)

and for
{\bm{h}}=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{B}}

\small\begin{split}\small|P[{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]{-}\widehat{P}_{\nnParams}[{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]|&\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]{-}\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\\
&\hskip 9.24994pt+\frac{2}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[{\bm{h}}]}\sqrt{\frac{P[{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{B}}]}{P[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}\Big[2(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})[A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}){+}A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{B}})]{+}\eta_{\mathbb{A}}\Big].\end{split}

(109)

Proof.

Leveraging the representations in (
103
) and (
105
) with
z=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}
, we get

\displaystyle{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]=\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\!+\!\tfrac{\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}-\tfrac{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\!+\!\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\left(\tfrac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}-\tfrac{1}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}\right)+\tfrac{\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}.

By triangular inequality applied to the norm in
{\mathcal{Z}}
, we get

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\!+\!\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}f({\mathbf{x}})]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\left|\tfrac{1}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}{-}\tfrac{1}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}\right|{+}\tfrac{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]}

\displaystyle\leq\!\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\!+\!\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\tfrac{2\eta_{\mathbb{A}}}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}+\tfrac{\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]-\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]},

where we have used Condition (
107
) in the last line to get that

\small\left|\frac{1}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}-\frac{1}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}]}\right|\leq\frac{\eta_{\mathbb{A}}}{(1-\eta_{\mathbb{A}})\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\leq\frac{2\eta_{\mathbb{A}}}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}.

From Proposition
L.3
and Condition (
107
) we get that

\displaystyle\small\tfrac{1}{\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}]}\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\!-\!\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}]]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\!\leq\!\tfrac{2(1\!+\!\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})\Big[A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}})A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})\Big]}{\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}})}\left\|\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}{}}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}{({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}

Cauchy’s Schwarz’s inequality again and
\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|\leq 1
give

\small\left\|\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\bm{h}}]({\mathbf{x}})]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq\left\|\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}{}}\left\|{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}{}}\leq\left\|\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}{}}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}{}}=\sqrt{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\muy}$}{({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}.

Combining the last four displays give the first result. The second result follows immediately for
{\bm{h}}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{B}}}
.
∎

Consequence of this result is that we can bound the error in probability as we can derive concentration inequalities on the terms in (
99
) and (
107
). Then an union bound gives the estimation result for regression conditional on sets.

Next, we recall that
\CE
being
(1/\alpha)
-Schatten class operator, implies:

Assumption L.5
.

Let there exist some constant
c>0
such that for
\alpha>0
, any
i\geq 1
and any
k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
, we have
\sigma_{i}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\leq c\,i^{-\alpha}
.

Further, for any
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
, we define
\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})={\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]
and

\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\bm{h}}):=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|-1}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\G^{\prime}\\
g\neq e\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[\langle\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}}),\overline{{\bm{h}}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})\rangle].

(110)

In the following, we consider observables
h
satisfying the following condition (that is clearly satisfied for an indicator of a set of positive measure)

Assumption L.6
.

Let there exists an absolute constant
C_{0}\geq 1
such that
(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}(h)\leq C_{0}\,\mathbb{E}[\left\|h({\mathbf{y}})\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}]
.

Define

\displaystyle\small\eta_{\mathbb{A}}=\eta_{\mathbb{A}}(\delta):=\left(\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}\right){\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}+\sqrt{2\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}.

Theorem L.7
.

Let Assumptions
L.6
and
L.5
be satisfied. Let
\mux
and
\muy
are
\G
-invariant, and
{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}
from (
89
) is
\G
-equivariant model, and let
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
and
{\bm{f}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})
(with values in
{\mathcal{Z}}
) be subGaussian random variables. Assume in addition that the event
{\mathbb{A}}
is anti-symmetric for
\G
and that
m_{k}=m
for all
k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
. Assume that
N\geq|\G|
.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds w.p.a.l
1-\delta

\small\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\lesssim_{C_{0}}\tfrac{\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}+\tfrac{\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1})}{(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}N)^{\frac{\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}}\right),

and

\small|\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{y}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}})-\widehat{\mathbb{P}}_{\nnParams}({\mathbf{y}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{B}}\mid{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}})|\lesssim_{C_{0}}\sqrt{\tfrac{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{y}}\in{\mathbb{B}}]}{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}+\tfrac{\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1})}{(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}N)^{\frac{\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}}+\sqrt{|\G|}\eta_{\mathbb{A}}\right).

Proof.

This result follows immediately from Propositions
L.3
and
L.4
combined with Lemmas
L.9
and Lemma
L.10
. Set

\displaystyle\small A({\bm{u}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{f}})

\displaystyle:=C\,\sqrt{\tfrac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|\,N}}\sqrt{C_{0}\vee\tfrac{|\G^{\prime}|}{N}}\,\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1}),

\displaystyle A({\bm{v}}_{\nnParams},{\bm{h}})

\displaystyle:=C\,\sqrt{\tfrac{\max_{k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\{m_{k}\}}{|\G^{\prime}|\,N}}\sqrt{C_{0}\vee\tfrac{|\G^{\prime}|}{N}}\,\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1}),

for some large enough absolute constant
C>0
.

Then an union bound based on Lemmas
L.9
and
L.10
guarantees that (
108
) is satisfied w.p.a.l.
1-\delta
(up to a rescaling of the constant
C
):

\small\begin{split}&\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq\\
&C\frac{\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{P[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}\Bigg[2(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})\Bigg(\sqrt{\tfrac{\max_{k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\{m_{k}\}}{|\G^{\prime}|\,N}}\sqrt{\,C_{0}\vee\tfrac{|\G^{\prime}|}{N}}\,\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1})\Bigg)\Bigg].\end{split}

Next we use our bound on the representation bias in (
96
)

\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{y}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{y}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq\left(\sigma_{{r_{m}}+1}^{\star}+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}\right)\frac{\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}\sqrt{\tfrac{1+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G}({\mathbb{A}})}{|\G^{\prime}|}}.

(111)

Recall that
\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}=\max_{k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\{\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\}
. Under Assumption
L.5
, we have
\left\|[\![{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}]\!]_{r_{m}}-{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}m)^{\alpha}}
. In addition,
(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G}({\mathbb{A}})\leq C_{0}
under Assumption
L.6
.

Combining the last two display gives w.p.a.l
1-\delta

\small\left\|\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}\!\in\!{\mathbb{A}}]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\lesssim_{C_{0}}\tfrac{\left\|{\bm{h}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})}}{\sqrt{{\mathbb{P}}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}\left(\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}+\tfrac{1}{(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}m)^{\alpha}}+\sqrt{\tfrac{m}{N}}\,\log(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1})\right).

Balancing the previous display w.r.t. dimension
m
, we get that
m\asymp(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}^{-2\alpha}N)^{\frac{1}{1+2\alpha}}
and the first result follows.

The bound for the conditional probability follows by picking
{\bm{y}}=\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{B}}}
.
∎

L.1
Quadratic Error Regression Bound

Our goal is to estimate the conditional expectation function

{\bm{z}}({\bm{x}})=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})|{\mathbf{x}}{=}{\bm{x}}]=\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]+[{\mathsf{D}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}$}}{\bm{h}}]({\bm{x}}).

Our estimator is

\widehat{{\bm{z}}}_{\nnParams}(\cdot)=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\mathbf{y}}]+[{\widehat{{\mathsf{D}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}}{\bm{h}}](\cdot).

Theorem L.8
.

Assume that
Y
is a sub-Gaussian random vector. Let Assumption
L.5
be satisfied. Assume in addition that
\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}\leq 1
,
m_{k}=m
for all
k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]
.
Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
such that
N\geq\,(c_{u}\vee c_{v})^{2}m\log(e\delta^{-1}{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}})\vee|\G|
, it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{z}}-\widehat{{\bm{z}}}_{\nnParams}\right\|^{2}_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{x}}}$}{}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\lesssim\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Cov}(Y))\left(\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{2}+(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}|\G|N)^{\frac{-2\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}\log^{2}(\delta^{-1}{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}})\right).

(112)

Discussion
When the training of the
NN
is successful, we expect the statistical rate to dominate the optimization error
\max_{k\in[{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}]}\{\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{{{\scalebox{0.6}{$({k})$}}}}\}
for large enough sample size
N
. For distribution containing symmetry invariants with large isotopic components (
m
is large), we observe that exploiting this information in the construction of the
NCP
operator yields a substantial improvement in the statistical error rate as we go from a rate

N^{-\frac{\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}
for standard
NCP
to
(N\,m)^{-\frac{\alpha}{1+2\alpha}}
for
eNCP
.

Proof.

Combining (
100
) with Lemma
L.9
gives w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\displaystyle\left\|{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\;{\mathbf{y}}-\widehat{{\mathsf{E}}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}\;{\mathbf{y}}\right\|_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}})}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim(1+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams})^{3}\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Cov}({\mathbf{y}}))\frac{m}{|\G|N}\log^{2}(2{n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1})

\displaystyle\lesssim\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Cov}({\mathbf{y}}))\frac{m}{|\G|N}\log^{2}({n_{{\text{\rm iso}}}}\delta^{-1}),

provide that
\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}\leq 1
.
We derived in (
94
) an upper bound on the bias term

\left\|\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}|{\mathbf{x}}}[{\mathbf{y}}\,|\,{\mathbf{x}}=\cdot]-{\mathsf{E}}_{\scalebox{0.65}{$\nnParams$}}{\mathbf{y}}\right\|^{2}_{\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})}\leq\mathrm{Tr}(\mathrm{Cov}({\mathbf{y}}))\left(\frac{1}{(d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}m)^{2\alpha}}+\mathcal{E}_{\nnParams}^{2}\right).

(113)

Balancing the two bounds in the last two displays w.r.t.
m\asymp(|\G|d_{{\text{\rm iso}}}N)^{\frac{1}{1+2\alpha}}
, we get the result.
∎

L.2
Auxiliary Results.

Consider the function space
\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
where
{\mathcal{Z}}
is endowed with an inner product
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle=\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{{\mathcal{Z}}}
. If the distribution of
{\mathbf{y}}
is
\G^{\prime}
-invariant, then for any
h\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
, we use the estimator
\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[h]
in (
97
) as an estimator of
\mathbb{E}[h({\mathbf{y}})]
.

Lemma L.9
.

Assume that the distribution
\muy
of
{\mathbf{y}}
is
\G
-invariant and let
\G^{\prime}\leq\G
. Let there exists a function
{\bm{h}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{{\mathbf{y}}}$}{}({\mathcal{Y}},{\mathcal{Z}})
such that
{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})
is subGaussian. Then there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq C\,\sqrt{\frac{\log^{2}2\delta^{-1}}{|\G^{\prime}|\,N}}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}]+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\bm{h}})+\frac{|\G^{\prime}|\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}]}{N}}.

Assume in addition that there exists an absolute constant
C_{0}\geq 1
such that
(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}(\overline{{\bm{h}}})\leq C_{0}\,\mathbb{E}[\left\|{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}^{2}]
. Then for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}\leq C\,\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}_{{\mathcal{Z}}}]}{|\G^{\prime}|\,N}}\sqrt{(1+C_{0})+\frac{|\G^{\prime}|}{N}}\,\log 2\delta^{-1}.

Note that similar bounds hold valid for the
\G
-invariant distribution
\mux
and any function
{\bm{f}}\in\scalebox{0.85}{$\mathcal{L}^{2}_{\mux}$}({\mathcal{X}},{\mathcal{Z}})
such that
{\bm{f}}({\mathbf{x}})
is subGaussian.

Proof.

We note that

\displaystyle\small\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}Z_{i}\quad\text{with }\quad Z_{i}=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\textnormal{y}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{{\textnormal{y}}_{i}}[{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\textnormal{y}}_{i})],\;\forall i\in[N].

Define

Z:=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})],

(114)

and, for brevity, set
\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|=\left\|{\bm{z}}\right\|_{{\mathcal{Z}}}=\sqrt{\langle{\bm{z}},{\bm{z}}\rangle_{{\mathcal{Z}}}}
for any
{\bm{z}}\in{\mathcal{Z}}
.
We apply Proposition
L.12
, to get w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}]-\mathbb{E}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]\right\|\leq\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbf{y}}}(\left\|Z\right\|)+\frac{\left\|Z\right\|_{\psi_{2}}^{2}}{N}}\log\frac{2}{\delta}.

Using the triangular inequality successively on
\left\|\cdot\right\|
and
\left\|\cdot\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
and the
\G^{\prime}
-invariance of
\muy
,
\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}=\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|_{\psi_{2}}
for any
g\in\G^{\prime}
, we get that

\left\|\left\|Z\right\|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\left\|\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}.

We note next that
\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|
is subGaussian. Consequently the well-known property of equivalence of moments for subGaussian distributions gives
\left\|Z\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\left\|\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|\right\|_{\psi_{2}}\lesssim\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}]
.
We derive now a control on
\mathrm{Var}_{{\mathbf{y}}}(\left\|Z\right\|)\leq\mathbb{E}[\left\|Z\right\|^{2}]
.
Using the
\G^{\prime}
-invariance of
\muy
, we get

\displaystyle\small\mathrm{Var}(\left\|Z\right\|)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}]}{|\G^{\prime}|}+\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}g\in\G^{\prime}\\
g\neq e\end{subarray}}\mathbb{E}[\langle{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})],{\bm{h}}(g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{Y}}$}}}}{\mathbf{y}})-\mathbb{E}[{\bm{h}}({\mathbf{y}})]\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}]}{|\G^{\prime}|}+\frac{(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\bm{h}})}{|\G^{\prime}|}\leq(1+C_{0})\frac{\mathbb{E}[\left\|\overline{{\bm{h}}}({\mathbf{y}})\right\|^{2}]}{|\G^{\prime}|}.

(115)

Hence we get the result.

∎

We focus now on a concentration bound for indicator functions
z=\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}
for any event
{\mathbb{A}}\in\Sigma_{{\mathcal{X}}}
. We define

\displaystyle\small Z_{A}

\displaystyle:=\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}]-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})])

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\big(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big)=\bigg(\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\bigg)-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}].

(116)

Note that we always have
|Z_{\mathbb{A}}|\leq 1
but this bound can be quite conservative as we could get a much sharper bound for some events
{\mathbb{A}}
. We denote by
\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})
the smallest deterministic upper-bound on

\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})

(For instance when
{\mathbb{A}}
is an antisymmetric event, then we have
\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})=1/|\G^{\prime}|
). Then we have

-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\leq Z_{\mathbb{A}}\leq\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}].

(117)

Define also

\displaystyle\small\Upsilon_{\G^{\prime},X}({\mathbb{A}})

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}})(1-\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}))+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\,\big(\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big)\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}].

(118)

Lemma L.10
.

Let the distribution of
{\mathbf{x}}
be
\G^{\prime}
-invariant. Then for any
{\mathbb{A}}\in\Sigma_{{\mathcal{X}}}
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|\leq\big|\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big|\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}+\sqrt{\frac{\Upsilon_{\G^{\prime},{\mathbf{x}}}({\mathbb{A}})}{|\G^{\prime}|}}\sqrt{2\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}.

Assume in addition that
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap{\mathbb{A}}=\emptyset
for all
g\in\G^{\prime}\setminus\{e\}
. Then it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small\frac{|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}\leq\left(\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}\right){\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}+\sqrt{2\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}.

If the distribution of
{\mathbf{y}}
is
\G^{\prime}
-invariant, then an identical result is immediately available for
{\mathbf{y}}
by the same proof argument.

Remark L.11
.

Using the standard empirical mean estimator that does not take advantage of
\G
-invariance, we obtain a concentration bound with a slower rate. For example, for an antisymmetric event
A
, we would achieve, w.p.a.l.
1-\delta
, the following result:

\small\frac{|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}\leq\left(\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}\right){\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}+\sqrt{2\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}}.

Specifically, leveraging
\G^{\prime}
-invariance allows us to replace
\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]
with
\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]
, which represents the probability of the entire orbit of
A
under the action of
\G^{\prime}
. This becomes particularly interesting when
\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\ll\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{${\mathcal{X}}$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]
, especially in the case of rare events where
\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\approx 0
.

Proof.

Since
\mux
is
\G^{\prime}
-invariant, we have
\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})]=\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]
and
\mathrm{Var}(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}))=\mathrm{Var}(\mathds{1}_{{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}))=\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}](1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}])
, for any
g\in\G^{\prime}
.
Hence

\displaystyle\small\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}Z_{i}\;\;\text{with}\;\;Z_{i}=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|}\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}_{i})-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}_{i})],\;\forall i\in[N].

The
Z_{i}
’s are i.i.d. copies of
Z=Z_{\mathbb{A}}
. In view of (
117
), we can apply Hoeffding’s inequality
Bercu et al. (2015, Theorem 2.16)
. We get for any
\delta\in(0,1)
w.p.a.l
1-\delta

\small|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|\leq\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})\sqrt{\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{2N}}.

(119)

We propose to prove another bound based on application of Bernstein’s inequality.
We first prove an improved bound on
\mathrm{Var}(Z)
as compared to the standard empirical mean estimator which does not exploit
\G
-invariance. Indeed we have

\displaystyle\small\mathrm{Var}(Z)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|^{2}}\left(\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime}}\mathrm{Var}(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}))+\sum_{g\neq g^{\prime}}\mathrm{Cov}\bigg(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}),\mathds{1}_{(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\bigg)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}})(1-\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}))}{|\G^{\prime}|}+\frac{1}{|\G^{\prime}|^{2}}\sum_{g\neq g^{\prime}}\mathrm{Cov}\bigg(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}),\mathds{1}_{(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\bigg).

Next, using again that
\mathbb{P}_{X}
is
\G
-invariant, we get for any
g,g^{\prime}\in\G^{\prime}

\displaystyle\small\mathrm{Cov}\bigg(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}),

\displaystyle\mathds{1}_{(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\bigg)=

(120)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]\,\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]^{2}.

(121)

Using again the invariance assumption, we note that

\displaystyle\small\sum_{g\neq g^{\prime}}\mathrm{Cov}\big(\mathds{1}_{g^{{-}1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}}),\mathds{1}_{(g^{\prime})^{-1}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}}({\mathbf{x}})\big)=|\G^{\prime}|\big(\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime},g\neq e}\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}\cap g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]\big)-|\G^{\prime}|(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]^{2}

Consequently by definition of
\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}(A)
in (
92
) and (
93
), we get

\small\sum_{g\in\G^{\prime},g\neq e}\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}\cap g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}A]=(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\,\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}(A)\,\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}).

Combining the last four displays, we get

\displaystyle\small\mathrm{Var}(Z)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}})(1-\mathbb{P}({\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}))+(|\G^{\prime}|-1)\,(\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}(A)-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}])\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}{|\G^{\prime}|}=\frac{\Upsilon_{\G^{\prime},{\mathbf{x}}}(A)}{|\G^{\prime}|}.

(122)

We note that for any
p\geq 3

\sum_{i=1}^{N}\mathbb{E}[\big(\max(0,Z_{i})\big)^{p}]\leq\frac{p!}{2}\max\big(0,\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big)^{p-2}\,N\,\mathrm{Var}(Z).

Then
Bercu et al. (2015, Theorem 2.1)
gives w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]\leq\max\big(0,\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big)\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(Z)}\sqrt{2\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}}.

Applying the same reasoning to variables
-Z_{1},\ldots,-Z_{N}
and an union bound gives gives w.p.a.l.
1-2\delta

\small|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|\leq\big|\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]\big|\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}+\sqrt{\mathrm{Var}(Z)}\sqrt{2\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}}.

(123)

Next, we note that when
g\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}\cap{\mathbb{A}}=\emptyset
for all
g\in\G^{\prime}\setminus\{e\}
, then
\gamma_{\G^{\prime}}({\mathbb{A}})=0
and
\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]=\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]/|\G^{\prime}|
. Consequently we get

\small\Upsilon_{\G^{\prime},{\mathbf{x}}}({\mathbb{A}})=\tfrac{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}](1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}])}{{\color[rgb]{0,0,0}|\G^{\prime}|}}\hskip 9.24994pt\text{and}\hskip 9.24994pt\tfrac{\gamma_{\G^{\prime},\infty}({\mathbb{A}})-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]}=\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}-1.

Hence under the additional assumptions, dividing by
\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]=\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in{\mathbb{A}}]
gives w.p.a.l.
1-2\delta

\small\frac{|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}-1\right){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}}+\sqrt{2\frac{\log\delta^{-1}}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}.

Replacing
\delta
by
\delta/2
gives w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\small\frac{|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{x}}}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}]-\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]|}{\mathbb{E}[\mathds{1}_{\mathbb{A}}({\mathbf{x}})]}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}-1\right){\color[rgb]{0,0,0}\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}+\sqrt{2\frac{\log 2\delta^{-1}}{N}}\sqrt{\frac{1-\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}{\mathbb{P}[{\mathbf{x}}\in\G^{\prime}\mathrel{{\triangleright_{\scalebox{0.65}{$$}}}}{\mathbb{A}}]}}.

(124)

∎

Proposition L.12
.

Let
A_{i}
,
i\in[N]
be i.i.d copies of a random variable
A
in a separable Hilbert space with norm
\left\|\cdot\right\|
. If there exist constants
L>0
and
\sigma>0
such that for every
m\geq 2
,
\mathbb{E}\left\|A\right\|^{m}\leq\frac{1}{2}m!L^{m-2}\sigma^{2}
,
then with probability at least
1-\delta

\left\|\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i\in[N]}A_{i}-\mathbb{E}A\right\|\leq\frac{4\sqrt{2}}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}+\frac{L^{2}}{N}}\log\frac{2}{\delta}.

(125)

Lemma L.13

((Sub-Gaussian random variable) Lemma 5.5. in
(
Vershynin, 2012
)
)
.

Let
Z
be a random variable. Then, the following assertions are equivalent with parameters
K_{i}>0
differing from each other by at most an absolute constant factor.

1.

Tails:

{\mathbb{P}}\{|Z|>t\}\leq\exp(1-t^{2}/K_{1}^{2})
for all
t\geq 0
;

2.

Moments:

(\mathbb{E}|Z|^{p})^{1/p}\leq K_{2}\sqrt{p}
for all
p\geq 1
;

3.

Super-exponential moment:

\mathbb{E}\exp(Z^{2}/K_{3}^{2})\leq 2
.

A random variable
Z
satisfying any of the above assertions is called a sub-Gaussian random variable. We will denote by
K_{3}
the sub-Gaussian norm.

Consequently, a sub-Gaussian random variable satisfies the following equivalence of moments property. There exists an absolute constant
c>0
such that for any
m\geq 2
,

\big(\mathbb{E}|Z|^{m}\big)^{1/m}\leq cK_{3}\sqrt{m}\big(\mathbb{E}|Z|^{2}\big)^{1/2}.

Lemma L.14
.

Assume that
Y
is sub-Gaussian with sub-Gaussian norm
K
.
We set
\sigma^{2}_{\theta}(Y):=\mathrm{Var}(\left\|Y-\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{y}}]\right\|)
. Then there exists an absolute constant
C>0
such that for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, it holds w.p.a.l.
1-\delta

\left\|\widehat{\mathbb{E}}_{{\mathbf{y}}}[{\mathbf{y}}]-\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{y}}]\right\|\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{N}}\sqrt{\sigma^{2}({\mathbf{y}})+\frac{K^{2}}{N}}\log(2\delta^{-1}).

Proof.

Set
Z:=\left\|{\mathbf{y}}-\mathbb{E}{\mathbf{y}}\right\|
and we recall that
\sigma^{2}({\mathbf{y}}):=\mathrm{Var}(\left\|{\mathbf{y}}-\mathbb{E}[{\mathbf{y}}]\right\|)
. We check that the moment condition,

\mathbb{E}Z^{m}\leq\frac{1}{2}m!L^{m-2}\sigma^{2}({\mathbf{y}})^{2},\quad\forall m\geq 2,

for some constant
L>0
to be specified.

The condition is obviously satisfied for
m=2
. Next for any
m\geq 3
, the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the equivalence of moment property give

\mathbb{E}Z^{m}\leq\left(\mathbb{E}Z^{2(m-2)}\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}Z^{4}\right)^{1/2}\leq 4K_{3}^{2}\sigma^{2}_{\theta}(Y)^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}Z^{2(m-2)}\right)^{1/2}.

Next, by homogeneity, rescaling
Z
to
Z/K_{1}
we can assume that
K_{1}=1
in Lemma
L.13
. We recall that if
Z
is in addition non-negative random variable, then for every integer
p\geq 1
, we have

\mathbb{E}Z^{p}=\int_{0}^{\infty}{\mathbb{P}}\{Z\geq t\}\,pt^{p-1}\,dt\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}e^{1-t^{2}}pt^{p-1}\,dt=\big(\frac{ep}{2}\big)\Gamma\big(\frac{p}{2}\big).

With
p=2(m-2)
, we get that
\mathbb{E}Z^{p}\leq e(m-2)\Gamma\big(m-2\big)=e(m-2)!=em!/2.

Using again Lemma
L.13
, we can take
L=cK
for some large enough absolute constant
c>0
. Then Proposition
L.12
gives the result.

∎
